          <p>(g) If the board prevails in an action brought under this subdivision and the court makes a finding that the misuse of funds was willful, the court may enter judgment in favor of the board and against the person misusing the funds in the amount of the misused funds.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.10A.022.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/470/#laws.0.2.0">1974 c 470 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/271/#laws.0.6.0">1975 c 271 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/307/#laws.0.8.0">1976 c 307 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/463/#laws.0.23.0">1978 c 463 s 23</a>-25; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/214/#laws.0.1.0">1987 c 214 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/349/#laws.0.5.0">1991 c 349 s 5</a>,6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/202/#laws.2.63.0">1997 c 202 art 2 s 63</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/220/#laws.0.2.0">1999 c 220 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/363/#laws.0.2.0">2002 c 363 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/138/#laws.1.11.0">2013 c 138 art 1 s 11</a>-13; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/309/#laws.0.6.0">2014 c 309 s 6</a>-8,16; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/73/#laws.0.1.0">2015 c 73 s 1</a>,5,26; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2018/0/119/#laws.0.5.0">2018 c 119 s 5</a>-9; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/0/50/#laws.1.2.0">2019 c 50 art 1 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/62/#laws.5.11.0">2023 c 62 art 5 s 11</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.10A.025">
        <h1 class="shn">10A.025 FILING REQUIREMENTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.025.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Filing date.</span></h2>
          <p>If a scheduled filing date under this chapter falls on a Saturday, Sunday, or legal holiday, the filing date is the next regular business day.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.025.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.<span class="headnote">Electronic filing.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A report or statement required to be filed under this chapter may be filed electronically. The board shall adopt rules to regulate electronic filing and to ensure that the electronic filing process is secure.</p>
          <p>(b) A document filed by facsimile transmission or electronic filing system has the same force and effect as filing an original paper document.</p>
          <p>(c) In order to provide a secure environment for the submission of electronic files, the board must require that a filer use a personal identification code when submitting an electronic file. The board may also request the filer to provide a valid email address in order to receive confirmation and verification messages from the board.</p>
          <p>(d) After an electronic file is processed by the board, the information contained in the electronic file becomes the property of the state subject to the terms of the Data Practices Act under chapter 13.</p>
          <p>(e) In the case of a filing by facsimile transmission, the filer must retain the original of the filed document and a record of the date and time of the transmission. If an electronic filing system is used to submit an electronic file to the board, the filer must retain as documentation the database and information on which the electronic submission of data is based. The database and records are subject to audit as provided in this chapter.</p>
          <p>(f) Within five days of a request by the board, any person filing a document by facsimile transmission or electronic filing system shall refile the document by one of the other filing methods provided in Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/4501.0500">4501.0500</a>, subpart 1.</p>
          <p>(g) Technical problems that prevent the successful submission of a facsimile transmission or electronic file do not relieve the filer of the responsibility of meeting the requirements of this chapter. An audit trail that demonstrates that the facsimile transmission or electronic file was successfully submitted in a timely fashion may be used by the board to waive late filing fees.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.025.1b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1b.<span class="headnote">Completion of filing.</span></h2>
          <p>A filing with the board is complete upon:</p>
          <p>(1) receipt in the board office of the document being filed, bearing the original signature of the person responsible for filing the document;</p>
          <p>(2) receipt of a facsimile transmission of the document, subject to subdivision 1a;</p>
          <p>(3) the postmark date of a first class or certified mailing of the document being filed, properly addressed to the board at its current address; or</p>
          <p>(4) the successful submission of an electronic file to the board.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.025.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Penalty for false statements.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A report or statement required to be filed under this chapter must be signed and certified as true by the individual required to file the report. The signature may be an electronic signature consisting of a password assigned by the board.</p>
          <p>(b) An individual shall not sign and certify to be true a report or statement knowing it contains false information or knowing it omits required information.</p>
          <p>(c) An individual shall not knowingly provide false or incomplete information to a treasurer with the intent that the treasurer will rely on that information in signing and certifying to be true a report or statement.</p>
          <p>(d) A person who violates paragraph (b) or (c) is subject to a civil penalty imposed by the board of up to $3,000. A violation of paragraph (b) or (c) is a gross misdemeanor.</p>
          <p>(e) The board may impose an additional civil penalty of up to $3,000 on the principal campaign committee or candidate, party unit, political committee, or association that has a political fund that is affiliated with an individual who violated paragraph (b) or (c).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.025.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Record keeping; penalty.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A person required to file a report or statement or who has accepted record-keeping responsibility for the filer must maintain records on the matters required to be reported, including vouchers, canceled checks, bills, invoices, worksheets, and receipts, that will provide in sufficient detail the necessary information from which the filed reports and statements may be verified, explained, clarified, and checked for accuracy and completeness. The person must keep the records available for audit, inspection, or examination by the board or its authorized representatives for four years from the date of filing of the reports or statements or of changes or corrections to them.</p>
          <p>(b) The board may impose a civil penalty of up to $3,000 on a person who knowingly violates this subdivision. The board may impose a separate civil penalty of up to $3,000 on the principal campaign committee or candidate, party unit, political committee, or association that has a political fund that is affiliated with an individual who violated this subdivision.</p>
          <p>(c) A knowing violation of this subdivision is a misdemeanor.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.025.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Changes and corrections.</span></h2>
          <p>Material changes in information previously submitted and corrections to a report or statement must be reported in writing to the board within ten days following the date of the event prompting the change or the date upon which the person filing became aware of the inaccuracy. The change or correction must identify the form and the paragraph containing the information to be changed or corrected. A request from the board to a lobbyist to provide more detailed information about a specific subject of interest disclosed on a lobbyist disbursement report is a change or correction governed by this subdivision.</p>
          <p>A person who willfully fails to report a material change or correction is subject to a civil penalty imposed by the board of up to $3,000. A willful violation of this subdivision is a gross misdemeanor.</p>
          <p>The board must send a written notice to any individual who fails to file a report required by this subdivision. If the individual fails to file the required report within ten business days after the notice was sent, the board may impose a late filing fee of $25 per day up to $1,000 starting on the 11th day after the notice was sent. The board may send an additional notice by certified mail to an individual who fails to file a report within ten business days after the first notice was sent by the board. The certified notice must state that if the individual does not file the requested report within ten business days after the certified notice was sent, the individual may be subject to a civil penalty for failure to file a report. An individual who fails to file a report required by this subdivision within ten business days after the certified notice was sent by the board is subject to a civil penalty imposed by the board of up to $1,000.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.025.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Reconciliation information; penalty.</span></h2>
          <p>An individual or association required to file a report under this chapter must provide information requested by the board to reconcile discrepancies between the report and reports filed by other individuals or associations. The board's request for information must be in writing. If the individual or association fails to provide the requested information within ten business days after the request was sent, the board may impose a late filing fee of $25 per day up to $1,000.</p>
          <p>The board may send notice by certified mail to an individual or association that has not timely responded to the board's written request for reconciliation information. The certified notice must state that if the individual or association does not respond to the board's request for information within ten business days after the certified notice was sent, the individual or association may be subject to a civil penalty for failure to provide information to the board. An individual or association that does not provide the requested information within ten business days after the certified notice was sent is subject to a civil penalty imposed by the board of up to $1,000.</p>
          <p>A person who willfully fails to cooperate with the board to reconcile a report discrepancy is subject to a civil penalty imposed by the board of up to $3,000.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.10A.025.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/470/#laws.0.10.0">1974 c 470 s 10</a>,22,23; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/271/#laws.0.6.0">1975 c 271 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/307/#laws.0.20.0">1976 c 307 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/463/#laws.0.38.0">1978 c 463 s 38</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/608/#laws.3.8.0">1990 c 608 art 3 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/220/#laws.0.3.0">1999 c 220 s 3</a>,13,24,26,50; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/363/#laws.0.3.0">2002 c 363 s 3</a>,4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/1/#laws.2.20.0">1Sp2003 c 1 art 2 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/156/#laws.6.3.0">2005 c 156 art 6 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/138/#laws.1.16.0">2013 c 138 art 1 s 16</a>,17; art 3 s 2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/309/#laws.0.11.0">2014 c 309 s 11</a>,12; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/4/#laws.3.3.0">1Sp2017 c 4 art 3 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2018/0/119/#laws.0.10.0">2018 c 119 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/62/#laws.5.12.0">2023 c 62 art 5 s 12</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.10A.027">
        <h1 class="shn">10A.027 INFORMATION ON WEBSITE.</h1>
        <p>The board must not post on its website any canceled checks, bank account numbers, credit card account numbers, or Social Security numbers that may be in the board's possession as a result of report or statement filings, complaints, or other proceedings under this chapter.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.10A.027.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/253/#laws.0.1.0">2006 c 253 s 1</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">LOBBYIST AND PRINCIPAL REPORTS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.10A.03">
        <h1 class="shn">10A.03 LOBBYIST REGISTRATION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.03.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">First registration.</span></h2>
          <p>A lobbyist must file a registration form with the board within five days after becoming a lobbyist or being engaged by a new individual, association, political subdivision, or public higher education system.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.03.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Form.</span></h2>
          <p>The board must prescribe a registration form, which must include:</p>
          <p>(1) the name, address, and email address of the lobbyist;</p>
          <p>(2) the principal place of business of the lobbyist;</p>
          <p>(3) the name and address of each individual, association, political subdivision, or public higher education system, if any, by whom the lobbyist is retained or employed or on whose behalf the lobbyist appears;</p>
          <p>(4) the website address of each association, political subdivision, or public higher education system identified under clause (3), if the entity maintains a website;</p>
          <p>(5) the general lobbying categories on which the lobbyist expects to lobby on behalf of a represented entity; and</p>
          <p>(6) if the lobbyist lobbies on behalf of an association, the name and address of the officers and directors of the association.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.03.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Failure to file.</span></h2>
          <p> If a lobbyist fails to file a registration form by the date that the form was due, the board may impose a late filing fee of $25 per day, not to exceed $1,000, starting on the day after the form was due. The board must send notice by certified mail to a lobbyist who fails to file a form within ten business days after the form was due that the lobbyist may be subject to a civil penalty for failure to file the form. A lobbyist who fails to file a form within seven days after the certified mail notice was sent by the board is subject to a civil penalty imposed by the board of up to $1,000.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.03.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Publication.</span></h2>
          <p>The restrictions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10.60">10.60</a> notwithstanding, the board may publish the information required in subdivision 2 on its website.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.03.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Exemptions.</span></h2>
          <p>For good cause shown, the board must grant exemptions to the requirement that email addresses be provided.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.03.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">General lobbying categories and specific subjects of interest.</span></h2>
          <p>A list of general lobbying categories and specific subjects of interest must be specified by the board and updated periodically based on public comment and information provided by lobbyists. The board must publish on its website the current list of general lobbying categories and specific subjects of interest. Chapter 14 and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.386">14.386</a> do not apply to the specification, publication, or periodic updates of the list of general lobbying categories and specific subjects of interest.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.10A.03.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/470/#laws.0.3.0">1974 c 470 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/271/#laws.0.6.0">1975 c 271 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/463/#laws.0.28.0">1978 c 463 s 28</a>,29; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/220/#laws.0.4.0">1999 c 220 s 4</a>,50; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/363/#laws.0.5.0">2002 c 363 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/1/#laws.2.21.0">1Sp2003 c 1 art 2 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/327/#laws.0.5.0">2010 c 327 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/73/#laws.0.2.0">2015 c 73 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/62/#laws.5.13.0">2023 c 62 art 5 s 13</a>,14</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.10A.04">
        <h1 class="shn">10A.04 LOBBYIST REPORTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.04.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Reports required.</span></h2>
          <p>A lobbyist must file reports of the lobbyist's activities with the board as long as the lobbyist continues to lobby. The report may be filed electronically. A lobbyist may file a termination statement at any time after ceasing to lobby.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.04.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Time of reports.</span></h2>
          <p>Each report must cover the time from the last day of the period covered by the last report to 15 days before the current filing date. The reports must be filed with the board by the following dates:</p>
          <p>(1) January 15; and</p>
          <p>(2) June 15.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.04.2a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2a.</h2>
          <p>MS 2003 Supp [Expired, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/1/#laws.2.24.0">1Sp2003 c 1 art 2 s 24</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.04.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Information to lobbyist.</span></h2>
          <p>An entity or lobbyist whose activities are reported to the board by another lobbyist is required to provide the information required by subdivision 4 to the lobbyist no later than five days before the prescribed filing date.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.04.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Content.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A report under this section must include information the board requires from the registration form and the information required by this subdivision for the reporting period.</p>
          <p>(b) A lobbyist must report the specific subjects of interest for an entity represented by the lobbyist on each report submitted under this section. A lobbyist must describe a specific subject of interest in the report with enough information to show the particular issue of importance to the entity represented.</p>
          <p>(c) A lobbyist must report every state agency that had administrative action that the represented entity sought to influence during the reporting period. The lobbyist must report the specific subjects of interest for each administrative action and the revisor of statutes rule draft number assigned to the administrative rulemaking.</p>
          <p>(d) A lobbyist must report every political subdivision that considered official action that the represented entity sought to influence during the reporting period. The lobbyist must report the specific subjects of interest for each action.</p>
          <p>(e) A lobbyist must report general lobbying categories and up to four specific subjects of interest related to each general lobbying category on which the lobbyist attempted to influence legislative action during the reporting period. If the lobbyist attempted to influence legislative action on more than four specific subjects of interest for a general lobbying category, the lobbyist, in consultation with the represented entity, must determine which four specific subjects of interest were the entity's highest priorities during the reporting period and report only those four subjects.</p>
          <p>(f) A lobbyist must report the Public Utilities Commission project name for each rate setting, power plant and powerline siting, or granting of certification of need before the Public Utilities Commission that the represented entity sought to influence during the reporting period.</p>
          <p>(g) A lobbyist must report the amount and nature of each gift, item, or benefit, excluding contributions to a candidate, equal in value to $5 or more, given or paid to any official, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10A.071#stat.10A.071.1">10A.071, subdivision 1</a>, by the lobbyist or an employer or employee of the lobbyist. The list must include the name and address of each official to whom the gift, item, or benefit was given or paid and the date it was given or paid.</p>
          <p>(h) A lobbyist must report each original source of money in excess of $500 in any year used for the purpose of lobbying to influence legislative action, administrative action, or the official action of a political subdivision. The list must include the name, address, and employer, or, if self-employed, the occupation and principal place of business, of each payer of money in excess of $500.</p>
          <p>(i) On each report, a lobbyist must disclose the general lobbying categories that were lobbied on in the reporting period.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.04.4a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4a.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed by amendment, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/220/#laws.0.5.0">1999 c 220 s 5</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.04.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Late filing.</span></h2>
          <p> If a lobbyist or principal fails to file a report required by this section by the date the report was due, the board may impose a late filing fee of $25 per day, not to exceed $1,000, commencing the day after the report was due. The board must send notice by certified mail to any lobbyist or principal who fails to file a report within ten business days after the report was due that the lobbyist or principal may be subject to a civil penalty for failure to file the report or pay the fee. A lobbyist or principal who fails to file a report or statement or pay a fee within seven days after the certified mail notice was sent by the board is subject to a civil penalty imposed by the board of up to $1,000.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.04.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Principal reports.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A principal must report to the board as required in this subdivision by March 15 for the preceding calendar year.</p>
          <p>(b) The principal must report the total amount, rounded to the nearest $5,000, spent by the principal during the preceding calendar year on each type of lobbying listed below:</p>
          <p>(1) lobbying to influence legislative action;</p>
          <p>(2) lobbying to influence administrative action, other than lobbying described in clause (3);</p>
          <p>(3) lobbying to influence administrative action in cases of rate setting, power plant and powerline siting, and granting of certificates of need under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.243">216B.243</a>; and</p>
          <p>(4) lobbying to influence official action of a political subdivision.</p>
          <p>(c) For each type of lobbying listed in paragraph (b), the principal must report a total amount that includes:</p>
          <p>(1) the portion of all direct payments for compensation and benefits paid by the principal to lobbyists in this state for that type of lobbying;</p>
          <p>(2) the portion of all expenditures for advertising, mailing, research, consulting, surveys, expert testimony, studies, reports, analysis, compilation and dissemination of information, communications and staff costs used for the purpose of urging members of the public to contact public or local officials to influence official actions, social media and public relations campaigns, and legal counsel used to support that type of lobbying in this state; and</p>
          <p>(3) a reasonable good faith estimate of the portion of all salaries and administrative overhead expenses attributable to activities of the principal for that type of lobbying in this state.</p>
          <p>(d) The principal must report disbursements made and obligations incurred that exceed $2,000 for paid advertising used for the purpose of urging members of the public to contact public or local officials to influence official actions during the reporting period. Paid advertising includes the cost to boost the distribution of an advertisement on social media. The report must provide the date that the advertising was purchased, the name and address of the vendor, a description of the advertising purchased, and any specific subjects of interest addressed by the advertisement.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.04.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Financial records.</span></h2>
          <p>The board may randomly audit the financial records of lobbyists and principals required to report under this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.04.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed by amendment, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/220/#laws.0.5.0">1999 c 220 s 5</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.04.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Reporting by multiple lobbyists representing the same entity.</span></h2>
          <p>Clauses (1) to (6) apply when a single individual, association, political subdivision, or public higher education system is represented by more than one lobbyist.</p>
          <p>(1) The entity must appoint one designated lobbyist to report lobbyist disbursements made by the entity. An entity represented by more than one lobbyist may only have one designated lobbyist at any given time. The designated lobbyist must indicate that status on the periodic reports of lobbyist disbursements.</p>
          <p>(2) A reporting lobbyist may consent to report on behalf of one or more other lobbyists for the same entity, in which case, the other lobbyists are persons whose activities the reporting lobbyist must disclose and are subject to the disclosure requirements of subdivision 3. Lobbyist disbursement reports filed by a reporting lobbyist must include the names and registration numbers of the other lobbyists whose activities are included in the report.</p>
          <p>(3) Lobbyists whose activities are accounted for by a reporting lobbyist are not required to file lobbyist disbursement reports.</p>
          <p>(4) A lobbyist whose lobbying disbursements are provided to the board through a reporting lobbyist must supply all relevant information on disbursements to the reporting lobbyist no later than five days before the prescribed filing date.</p>
          <p>(5) The reporting periods and due dates for a reporting lobbyist are those provided in subdivision 2. The late filing provisions in subdivision 5 apply to reports required by this subdivision.</p>
          <p>(6) The reporting lobbyist must indicate the names and registration numbers of any lobbyists who did not provide their lobbying disbursements for inclusion in a report. The late filing provisions in subdivision 5 apply to lobbyists who fail to report information to the reporting lobbyist.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.10A.04.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/470/#laws.0.4.0">1974 c 470 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/271/#laws.0.6.0">1975 c 271 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/307/#laws.0.9.0">1976 c 307 s 9</a>,10; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/463/#laws.0.30.0">1978 c 463 s 30</a>-32; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/654/#laws.2.37.0">1984 c 654 art 2 s 37</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/1/3/#laws.1.3.0">1Sp1986 c 3 art 1 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/608/#laws.1.7.0">1990 c 608 art 1 s 7</a>-11; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/318/#laws.2.5.0">1993 c 318 art 2 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/377/#laws.0.2.0">1994 c 377 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/220/#laws.0.5.0">1999 c 220 s 5</a>,50; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/363/#laws.0.6.0">2002 c 363 s 6</a>-8; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/1/#laws.2.22.0">1Sp2003 c 1 art 2 s 22</a>-27; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/23/#laws.0.10.0">1Sp2003 c 23 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/10/#laws.1.2.0">2005 c 10 art 1 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/327/#laws.0.6.0">2010 c 327 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/251/#laws.0.1.0">2012 c 251 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/138/#laws.3.3.0">2013 c 138 art 3 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/4/#laws.3.4.0">1Sp2017 c 4 art 3 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/62/#laws.5.15.0">2023 c 62 art 5 s 15</a>-18; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/112/#laws.4.6.0">2024 c 112 art 4 s 6</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.10A.05">
        <h1 class="shn">10A.05 LOBBYIST REPORT.</h1>
        <p>Within 30 days after each lobbyist filing date set by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10A.04">10A.04</a>, the executive director of the board must publish the names of the lobbyists registered who were not previously reported, the names of the individuals, associations, political subdivisions, or public higher education systems whom they represent as lobbyists, the subject or subjects on which they are lobbying, and whether in each case they lobby to influence legislative action, administrative action, or the official action of a political subdivision.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.10A.05.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/470/#laws.0.5.0">1974 c 470 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/271/#laws.0.6.0">1975 c 271 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/608/#laws.1.12.0">1990 c 608 art 1 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/220/#laws.0.6.0">1999 c 220 s 6</a>,50; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/62/#laws.5.19.0">2023 c 62 art 5 s 19</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.10A.06">
        <h1 class="shn">10A.06 CONTINGENT FEES PROHIBITED.</h1>
        <p>No person may act as or employ a lobbyist for compensation that is dependent upon the result or outcome of any legislative or administrative action, or of the official action of a political subdivision. A person who violates this section is guilty of a gross misdemeanor.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.10A.06.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/470/#laws.0.6.0">1974 c 470 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/608/#laws.1.13.0">1990 c 608 art 1 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/220/#laws.0.7.0">1999 c 220 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/62/#laws.5.20.0">2023 c 62 art 5 s 20</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr_by_subd" id="stat.10A.065">
        <h1>10A.065 </h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.065.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10A.273#stat.10A.273.1">10A.273, subdivision 1</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.065.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10A.273">10A.273, subd 2</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.065.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10A.273">10A.273, subd 3</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.065.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10A.273">10A.273, subd 4</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.065.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10A.273">10A.273, subd 5</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.065.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/220/#laws.0.51.0">1999 c 220 s 51</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">PUBLIC OFFICIALS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.10A.07">
        <h1 class="shn">10A.07 CONFLICTS OF INTEREST.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.07.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Disclosure of potential conflicts.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A public official or a local official elected to or appointed by a metropolitan governmental unit who in the discharge of official duties would be required to take an action or make a decision that would substantially affect the official's financial interests or those of an associated business, unless the effect on the official is no greater than on other members of the official's business classification, profession, or occupation, must take the following actions:</p>
          <p>(1) prepare a written statement describing the matter requiring action or decision and the nature of the potential conflict of interest;</p>
          <p>(2) deliver copies of the statement to the official's immediate superior, if any; and</p>
          <p>(3) if a member of the legislature or of the governing body of a metropolitan governmental unit, deliver a copy of the statement to the presiding officer of the body of service.</p>
          <p>If a potential conflict of interest presents itself and there is insufficient time to comply with clauses (1) to (3), the public or local official must orally inform the superior or the official body of service or committee of the body of the potential conflict.</p>
          <p>(b) For purposes of this section, "financial interest" means any ownership or control in an asset that has the potential to produce a monetary return.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.07.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Required actions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) If the official is not a member of the legislature or of the governing body of a metropolitan governmental unit, the superior must assign the matter, if possible, to another employee who does not have a potential conflict of interest.</p>
          <p>(b) If there is no immediate superior, the official must abstain, if possible, by assigning the matter to a subordinate for disposition or requesting the appointing authority to designate another to determine the matter. The official shall not chair a meeting, participate in any vote, or offer any motion or discussion on the matter giving rise to the potential conflict of interest.</p>
          <p>(c) If the official is a member of the legislature, the house of service may, at the member's request, excuse the member from taking part in the action or decision in question.</p>
          <p>(d) If an official is not permitted or is otherwise unable to abstain from action in connection with the matter, the official must file a statement describing the potential conflict and the action taken. A public official must file the statement with the board and a local official must file the statement with the governing body of the official's political subdivision. The statement must be filed within a week of the action taken.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.07.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Interest in contract; local officials.</span></h2>
          <p>This section does not apply to a local official with respect to a matter governed by sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/471.87">471.87</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/471.88">471.88</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.07.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Exception; judges.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding subdivisions 1 and 2, a public official who is a district court judge, an appeals court judge, or a supreme court justice is not required to comply with the provisions of this section.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.10A.07.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/470/#laws.0.7.0">1974 c 470 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/271/#laws.0.6.0">1975 c 271 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/463/#laws.0.33.0">1978 c 463 s 33</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/608/#laws.2.1.0">1990 c 608 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/220/#laws.0.50.0">1999 c 220 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/138/#laws.2.2.0">2013 c 138 art 2 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2018/0/119/#laws.0.11.0">2018 c 119 s 11</a>,12</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.10A.071">
        <h1 class="shn">10A.071 CERTAIN GIFTS BY LOBBYISTS AND PRINCIPALS PROHIBITED.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.071.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The definitions in this subdivision apply to this section.</p>
          <p>(b) "Gift" means money, real or personal property, a service, a loan, a forbearance or forgiveness of indebtedness, or a promise of future employment, that is given and received without the giver receiving consideration of equal or greater value in return.</p>
          <p>(c) "Official" means a public official, an employee of the legislature, or a local official.</p>
          <p>(d) "Plaque" means a decorative item with an inscription recognizing an individual for an accomplishment.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.071.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Prohibition.</span></h2>
          <p>A lobbyist or principal may not give a gift or request another to give a gift to an official. An official may not accept a gift from a lobbyist or principal.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.071.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Exceptions.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) The prohibitions in this section do not apply if the gift is:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) a contribution as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10A.01#stat.10A.01.11">10A.01, subdivision 11</a>;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) services to assist an official in the performance of official duties, including but not limited to providing advice, consultation, information, and communication in connection with legislation, and services to constituents;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) services of insignificant monetary value;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) a plaque with a resale value of $5 or less;</p>
          <p class="in">(5) a trinket or memento costing $5 or less;</p>
          <p class="in">(6) informational material with a resale value of $5 or less; or</p>
          <p class="in">(7) food or a beverage given at a reception, meal, or meeting if:</p>
          <p>(i) the reception, meal, or meeting is held away from the recipient's place of work by an organization before whom the recipient appears to make a speech or answer questions as part of a program; or</p>
          <p>(ii) the recipient is a member or employee of the legislature and an invitation to attend the reception, meal, or meeting was provided to all members of the legislature at least five days prior to the date of the event.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The prohibitions in this section do not apply if the gift is given:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) because of the recipient's membership in a group, a majority of whose members are not officials, and an equivalent gift is given to the other members of the group; or</p>
          <p class="in">(2) by a lobbyist or principal who is a member of the family of the recipient, unless the gift is given on behalf of someone who is not a member of that family.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.10A.071.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/377/#laws.0.5.0">1994 c 377 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/220/#laws.0.50.0">1999 c 220 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/156/#laws.6.4.0">2005 c 156 art 6 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/295/#laws.0.3.0">2008 c 295 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/327/#laws.0.7.0">2010 c 327 s 7</a>,8; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/138/#laws.1.18.0">2013 c 138 art 1 s 18</a>; art 2 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/4/#laws.3.5.0">1Sp2017 c 4 art 3 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/62/#laws.5.21.0">2023 c 62 art 5 s 21</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.10A.08">
        <h1 class="shn">10A.08 REPRESENTATION DISCLOSURE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.08.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Disclosure required.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A public official who represents a client for a fee before an individual, board, commission, or agency that has rulemaking authority in a hearing conducted under chapter <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14">14</a>, must disclose the official's participation in the action to the board within 14 days after the public official's initial appearance at a hearing. If the public official fails to disclose the participation by the date that the disclosure was due, the board may impose a late filing fee of $25 per day, not to exceed $1,000, starting on the day after the disclosure was due. The board must send notice by certified mail to a public official who fails to disclose the participation within ten business days after the disclosure was due that the public official may be subject to a civil penalty for failure to disclose the participation. A public official who fails to disclose the participation within seven days after the certified mail notice was sent by the board is subject to a civil penalty imposed by the board of up to $1,000.</p>
          <p>(b) A public official required to disclose representation under this section shall provide the following information: name, address, and office held; name and address of each client represented at the hearing; the name of the individual, board, commission, or agency conducting the hearing and the date and location of the initial appearance at the hearing; and a general description of the subject or subjects on which the public official represented the client in the hearing.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.08.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Exception; judges.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding subdivision 1, a public official who is a district court judge, an appeals court judge, or a supreme court justice is not required to comply with the provisions of this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.08.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For purposes of this section, the following definitions have the meanings given.</p>
          <p>(b) "Fee" means any compensation or other consideration for services performed or for future services.</p>
          <p>(c) "Initial appearance at a hearing" means the first appearance by a public official representing a client for a fee at a hearing on a single subject. Subsequent appearances at continuations of the same hearing are not initial appearances.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.10A.08.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/470/#laws.0.8.0">1974 c 470 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/271/#laws.0.6.0">1975 c 271 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/463/#laws.0.34.0">1978 c 463 s 34</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/424/#laws.0.130.0">1982 c 424 s 130</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/220/#laws.0.11.0">1999 c 220 s 11</a>,50; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/363/#laws.0.9.0">2002 c 363 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/156/#laws.6.5.0">2005 c 156 art 6 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/327/#laws.0.9.0">2010 c 327 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/138/#laws.2.4.0">2013 c 138 art 2 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/73/#laws.0.3.0">2015 c 73 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2018/0/119/#laws.0.13.0">2018 c 119 s 13</a>,14; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/0/50/#laws.1.3.0">2019 c 50 art 1 s 3</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.10A.09">
        <h1 class="shn">10A.09 STATEMENTS OF ECONOMIC INTEREST.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.09.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Time for filing.</span></h2>
          <p> An individual must file a statement of economic interest:</p>
          <p>(1) within 60 days of accepting employment as a public official or a local official in a metropolitan governmental unit;</p>
          <p>(2) within 60 days of assuming office as a district court judge, appeals court judge, supreme court justice, or county commissioner;</p>
          <p>(3) within 14 days after filing an affidavit of candidacy or petition to appear on the ballot for an elective state constitutional or legislative office or an elective local office in a metropolitan governmental unit other than county commissioner;</p>
          <p>(4) in the case of a public official requiring the advice and consent of the senate, within 14 days after undertaking the duties of office; or</p>
          <p>(5) in the case of members of the Minnesota Racing Commission, the director of the Minnesota Racing Commission, chief of security, medical officer, inspector of pari-mutuels, and stewards employed or approved by the commission or persons who fulfill those duties under contract, within 60 days of accepting or assuming duties.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.09.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.<span class="headnote">Exception; retired judges.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding subdivision 1, a retired judge or justice appointed to serve as a judge or justice under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/2.724">2.724</a> is not required to comply with the provisions of this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.09.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Notice to board.</span></h2>
          <p>The secretary of state or the appropriate county auditor, upon receiving an affidavit of candidacy or petition to appear on the ballot from an individual required by this section to file a statement of economic interest, and any official who nominates or employs a public official required by this section to file a statement of economic interest, must notify the board of the name of the individual required to file a statement and the date of the affidavit, petition, or nomination.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.09.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Notice of filing.</span></h2>
          <p>The board must notify the presiding officer of the house that will approve or disapprove the nomination, of the name of an individual who has filed a statement of economic interest with the board, a copy of the statement, and the date on which the statement was filed.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.09.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/463/#laws.0.109.0">1978 c 463 s 109</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.09.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Form; general requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A statement of economic interest required by this section must be on a form prescribed by the board. Except as provided in subdivision 5b, the individual filing must provide the following information:</p>
          <p>(1) the individual's name, address, occupation, and principal place of business;</p>
          <p>(2) a listing of the name of each associated business and the nature of that association;</p>
          <p>(3) a listing of all real property within the state, excluding homestead property, in which the individual or the individual's spouse holds: (i) a fee simple interest, a mortgage, a contract for deed as buyer or seller, or an option to buy, whether direct or indirect, if the interest is valued in excess of $2,500; or (ii) an option to buy, if the property has a fair market value of more than $50,000;</p>
          <p>(4) a listing of all real property within the state in which a partnership of which the individual or the individual's spouse is a member holds: (i) a fee simple interest, a mortgage, a contract for deed as buyer or seller, or an option to buy, whether direct or indirect, if the individual's share of the partnership interest is valued in excess of $2,500; or (ii) an option to buy, if the property has a fair market value of more than $50,000. A listing under this clause or clause (3) must indicate the street address and the municipality or the section, township, range and approximate acreage, whichever applies, and the county in which the property is located;</p>
          <p>(5) a listing of any investments, ownership, or interests in property connected with pari-mutuel horse racing in the United States and Canada, including a racehorse, in which the individual directly or indirectly holds a partial or full interest or an immediate family member holds a partial or full interest;</p>
          <p>(6) a listing of the principal business or professional activity category of each business from which the individual or the individual's spouse receives more than $250 in any month during the reporting period as an employee, if the individual or the individual's spouse has an ownership interest of 25 percent or more in the business;</p>
          <p>(7) a listing of each principal business or professional activity category from which the individual or the individual's spouse received compensation of more than $2,500 in the past 12 months as an independent contractor;</p>
          <p>(8) a listing of the full name of each security with a value of more than $10,000 owned in part or in full by the individual or the individual's spouse, at any time during the reporting period; and</p>
          <p>(9) a listing of any contract, professional license, lease, or franchise that:</p>
          <p>(i) is held by the individual or the individual's spouse or any business in which the individual has an ownership interest of 25 percent or more; and</p>
          <p>(ii) is entered into with, or issued by, the government agency on which the individual serves as a public or local official.</p>
          <p>(b) The business or professional categories for purposes of paragraph (a), clauses (6) and (7), must be the general topic headings used by the federal Internal Revenue Service for purposes of reporting self-employment income on Schedule C. This paragraph does not require an individual to report any specific code number from that schedule. Any additional principal business or professional activity category may only be adopted if the category is enacted by law.</p>
          <p>(c) For the purpose of calculating the amount of compensation received from any single source in a single month, the amount shall include the total amount received from the source during the month, whether or not the amount covers compensation for more than one month.</p>
          <p>(d) For the purpose of determining the value of an individual's interest in real property, the value of the property is the market value shown on the property tax statement.</p>
          <p>(e) For the purpose of this section, "date of appointment" means the effective date of appointment to a position.</p>
          <p>(f) For the purpose of this section, "accepting employment as a public official" means the effective date of the appointment to the position, as stated in the appointing authority's notice to the board.</p>
          <p>(g) The listings required in paragraph (a), clauses (3) to (9), must not identify whether the individual or the individual's spouse is associated with or owns the listed item.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.09.5a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5a.<span class="headnote">Original statement; reporting period.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) An original statement of economic interest required under subdivision 1, clause (1), must cover the calendar month before the month in which the individual accepted employment as a public official or a local official in a metropolitan governmental unit.</p>
          <p>(b) An original statement of economic interest required under subdivision 1, clauses (2), (4), and (5), must cover the calendar month before the month in which the individual assumed or undertook the duties of office.</p>
          <p>(c) An original statement of economic interest required under subdivision 1, clause (3), must cover the calendar month before the month in which the candidate filed the affidavit of candidacy.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.09.5b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5b.<span class="headnote">Form; exceptions for certain officials.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) This subdivision applies to the following individuals:</p>
          <p>(1) a supervisor of a soil and water conservation district;</p>
          <p>(2) a manager of a watershed district; and</p>
          <p>(3) a member of a watershed management organization as defined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103B.205#stat.103B.205.13">103B.205, subdivision 13</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) Notwithstanding subdivision 5, paragraph (a), an individual listed in paragraph (a), must provide only the information listed below on a statement of economic interest:</p>
          <p>(1) the individual's name, address, occupation, and principal place of business;</p>
          <p>(2) a listing of any association, corporation, partnership, limited liability company, limited liability partnership, or other organized legal entity from which the individual receives compensation in excess of $250, except for actual and reasonable expenses, in any month during the reporting period as a director, officer, owner, member, partner, employer, or employee;</p>
          <p>(3) a listing of all real property within the state, excluding homestead property, in which the individual or the individual's spouse holds:</p>
          <p>(i) a fee simple interest, a mortgage, a contract for deed as buyer or seller, or an option to buy, whether direct or indirect, if the interest is valued in excess of $2,500; or</p>
          <p>(ii) an option to buy, if the property has a fair market value of more than $50,000;</p>
          <p>(4) a listing of all real property within the state in which a partnership of which the individual or the individual's spouse is a member holds:</p>
          <p>(i) a fee simple interest, a mortgage, a contract for deed as buyer or seller, or an option to buy, whether direct or indirect, if the individual's share of the partnership interest is valued in excess of $2,500; or</p>
          <p>(ii) an option to buy, if the property has a fair market value of more than $50,000. A listing under this clause or clause (3) must indicate the street address and the municipality or the section, township, range and approximate acreage, whichever applies, and the county in which the property is located; and</p>
          <p>(5) a listing of any contract, professional license, lease, or franchise that meets the following criteria:</p>
          <p>(i) it is held by the individual or the individual's spouse or any business in which the individual has an ownership interest of 25 percent or more; and</p>
          <p>(ii) it is entered into with, or issued by, the government agency on which the individual serves as a public or local official.</p>
          <p>(c) The listings required in paragraph (b), clauses (3) to (5), must not identify whether the individual or the individual's spouse is associated with or owns the listed item.</p>
          <p>(d) If an individual listed in paragraph (a) also holds a public official position that is not listed in paragraph (a), the individual must file a statement of economic interest that includes the information specified in subdivision 5, paragraph (a).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.09.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Annual statement.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Each individual who is required to file a statement of economic interest must also file an annual statement by the last Monday in January of each year that the individual remains in office. The annual statement must cover the period through December 31 of the year prior to the year when the statement is due. The annual statement must include the amount of each honorarium in excess of $250 received since the previous statement and the name and address of the source of the honorarium. The board must maintain each annual statement of economic interest submitted by an officeholder in the same file with the statement submitted as a candidate.</p>
          <p>(b) An individual must file the annual statement of economic interest required by this subdivision to cover the period for which the individual served as a public official even though at the time the statement was filed, the individual is no longer holding that office as a public official.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.09.6a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6a.<span class="headnote">Place of filing.</span></h2>
          <p>A public official required to file a statement under this section must file it with the board. A local official required to file a statement under this section must file it with the governing body of the official's political subdivision. The governing body must maintain statements filed with it under this subdivision as public data. If an official position is defined as both a public official and as a local official of a metropolitan governmental unit under this chapter, the official must file the statement with the board.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.09.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Late filing.</span></h2>
          <p> If an individual fails to file a statement of economic interest required by this section within ten business days after the statement was due, the board may impose a late filing fee of $5 per day, not to exceed $100, commencing on the 11th day after the statement was due. The board must send notice by certified mail to any individual who fails to file a statement within ten business days after the statement was due that the individual may be subject to a civil penalty for failure to file a statement. An individual who fails to file a statement within seven days after the certified mail notice was sent by the board is subject to a civil penalty imposed by the board up to $1,000.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.09.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/309/#laws.0.25.0">2014 c 309 s 25</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.09.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Waivers.</span></h2>
          <p>Upon written request and for good cause shown, the board may waive the requirement that an official disclose the address of real property that constitutes a secondary residence of the official.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.09.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered 10A.022, subd 6]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.10A.09.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/470/#laws.0.9.0">1974 c 470 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/271/#laws.0.6.0">1975 c 271 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/307/#laws.0.11.0">1976 c 307 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/463/#laws.0.35.0">1978 c 463 s 35</a>-37; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/424/#laws.0.130.0">1982 c 424 s 130</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/214/#laws.0.30.0">1983 c 214 s 30</a>,31; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/305/#laws.0.3.0">1983 c 305 s 3</a>,4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/334/#laws.6.2.0">1989 c 334 art 6 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/608/#laws.2.2.0">1990 c 608 art 2 s 2</a>-4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/233/#laws.0.109.0">1991 c 233 s 109</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/202/#laws.2.6.0">1997 c 202 art 2 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/220/#laws.0.12.0">1999 c 220 s 12</a>,50; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/363/#laws.0.10.0">2002 c 363 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/327/#laws.0.10.0">2010 c 327 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/138/#laws.2.5.0">2013 c 138 art 2 s 5</a>,6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/309/#laws.0.13.0">2014 c 309 s 13</a>-16; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/73/#laws.0.4.0">2015 c 73 s 4</a>,5,26; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/158/#laws.1.4.0">2016 c 158 art 1 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/4/#laws.3.6.0">1Sp2017 c 4 art 3 s 6</a>,7; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2018/0/119/#laws.0.15.0">2018 c 119 s 15</a>,16; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/31/#laws.4.14.0">2021 c 31 art 4 s 14</a>-18; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/62/#laws.5.22.0">2023 c 62 art 5 s 22</a>,23</p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.10A.10"><b>10A.10</b> [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10A.025">10A.025, subd 2</a>]
</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">CAMPAIGN FINANCE; ORGANIZATION AND REPORTING</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.10A.105">
        <h1 class="shn">10A.105 PRINCIPAL CAMPAIGN COMMITTEE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.105.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Single committee.</span></h2>
          <p>A candidate must not accept contributions from a source, other than self, in aggregate in excess of $750 or accept a public subsidy unless the candidate designates and causes to be formed a single principal campaign committee for each office sought. A candidate may not authorize, designate, or cause to be formed any other political committee bearing the candidate's name or title or otherwise operating under the direct or indirect control of the candidate. However, a candidate may be involved in the direct or indirect control of a party unit.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.105.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Replacement of officers.</span></h2>
          <p>A candidate may at any time without cause remove and replace the chair, treasurer, deputy treasurer, or any other officer of the candidate's principal campaign committee.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.10A.105.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/470/#laws.0.19.0">1974 c 470 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/307/#laws.0.13.0">1976 c 307 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/463/#laws.0.50.0">1978 c 463 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/318/#laws.2.14.0">1993 c 318 art 2 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/220/#laws.0.22.0">1999 c 220 s 22</a>,50; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/138/#laws.1.19.0">2013 c 138 art 1 s 19</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.10A.11">
        <h1 class="shn">10A.11 ORGANIZATION OF COMMITTEES AND PARTY UNITS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.11.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Chair and treasurer.</span></h2>
          <p>A political committee, principal campaign committee, or party unit must have a chair and a treasurer. The chair and treasurer may be the same individual.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.11.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Treasurer vacancy.</span></h2>
          <p>A political committee, principal campaign committee, or party unit may not accept a contribution or make an expenditure or permit an expenditure to be made on its behalf while the office of treasurer is vacant.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.11.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Deputy treasurers.</span></h2>
          <p>The treasurer of a political committee, principal campaign committee, or party unit may appoint as many deputy treasurers as necessary and is responsible for their accounts.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.11.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Depositories.</span></h2>
          <p>The treasurer of a political committee, principal campaign committee, or party unit may designate one or two depositories in each county in which a campaign is conducted.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.11.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Commingling prohibited.</span></h2>
          <p>A political committee, principal campaign committee, or party unit may not commingle its funds with personal funds of officers, members, or associates of the committee.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.11.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/463/#laws.0.109.0">1978 c 463 s 109</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.11.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Penalty.</span></h2>
          <p>A person who knowingly violates this section is subject to a civil penalty imposed by the board of up to $1,000.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.10A.11.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/470/#laws.0.11.0">1974 c 470 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/463/#laws.0.39.0">1978 c 463 s 39</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/220/#laws.0.14.0">1999 c 220 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/363/#laws.0.11.0">2002 c 363 s 11</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.10A.12">
        <h1 class="shn">10A.12 POLITICAL FUNDS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.12.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">When required for contributions and approved expenditures.</span></h2>
          <p>An association other than a political committee or party unit may not contribute more than $750 in aggregate in any calendar year to candidates, local candidates, political committees, or party units or make approved expenditures of more than $750 in aggregate in any calendar year unless the contribution or expenditure is made through a political fund.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.12.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.<span class="headnote">When required for independent expenditures or ballot questions.</span></h2>
          <p>An association other than a political committee that makes only independent expenditures or expenditures to promote or defeat a ballot question must do so through an independent expenditure or ballot question political fund if the independent expenditures aggregate more than $1,500 in a calendar year or if the expenditures to promote or defeat a ballot question aggregate more than $5,000 in a calendar year, or by contributing to an existing independent expenditure or ballot question political committee or fund.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.12.1b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1b.<span class="headnote">Penalty for noncompliant independent expenditure.</span></h2>
          <p>An association that makes an independent expenditure without complying with subdivision 1a is subject to a civil penalty of up to four times the amount of the independent expenditure, but not to exceed $25,000, except when the violation was intentional.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.12.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Commingling prohibited.</span></h2>
          <p>The contents of an association's political fund may not be commingled with other funds or with the personal funds of an officer or member of the association or the fund. It is not commingling for an association that uses only its own general treasury money to make expenditures and disbursements permitted under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10A.121#stat.10A.121.1">10A.121, subdivision 1</a>, directly from the depository used for its general treasury money. An association that accepts more than $1,500 in aggregate in contributions to influence the nomination or election of candidates or local candidates or more than $5,000 in contributions to promote or defeat a ballot question must establish a separate depository for those contributions.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.12.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Treasurer.</span></h2>
          <p>An association that has a political fund must elect or appoint a treasurer of the political fund.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.12.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Treasurer vacancy.</span></h2>
          <p>A political fund may not accept a contribution or make an expenditure or contribution from the political fund while the office of treasurer of the political fund is vacant.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.12.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Dues or membership fees.</span></h2>
          <p>An association may, if not prohibited by other law, deposit in its political fund money derived from dues or membership fees. Under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10A.20">10A.20</a>, the treasurer of the fund must disclose the name of any member whose dues, membership fees, and contributions deposited in the political fund together exceed $200 in a year.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.12.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Penalty.</span></h2>
          <p>A person who knowingly violates this section is subject to a civil penalty imposed by the board of up to $1,000.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.10A.12.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/470/#laws.0.12.0">1974 c 470 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/463/#laws.0.40.0">1978 c 463 s 40</a>-42; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/587/#laws.2.8.0">1980 c 587 art 2 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/607/#laws.17.9.0">1980 c 607 art 17 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/214/#laws.0.2.0">1987 c 214 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/220/#laws.0.15.0">1999 c 220 s 15</a>,50; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/363/#laws.0.12.0">2002 c 363 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/397/#laws.0.4.0">2010 c 397 s 4</a>,5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/138/#laws.1.20.0">2013 c 138 art 1 s 20</a>-22; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/309/#laws.0.17.0">2014 c 309 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/31/#laws.4.19.0">2021 c 31 art 4 s 19</a>,20</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.10A.121">
        <h1 class="shn">10A.121 INDEPENDENT EXPENDITURE AND BALLOT QUESTION POLITICAL COMMITTEES AND FUNDS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.121.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Permitted disbursements.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">An independent expenditure political committee or fund, or a ballot question political committee or fund, may:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) pay costs associated with its fundraising and general operations;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) pay for communications that do not constitute contributions or approved expenditures;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) make contributions to independent expenditure or ballot question political committees or funds;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) make independent expenditures;</p>
          <p class="in">(5) make expenditures to promote or defeat ballot questions;</p>
          <p class="in">(6) return a contribution to its source;</p>
          <p class="in">(7) for a political fund, record bookkeeping entries transferring the association's general treasury money allocated for political purposes back to the general treasury of the association;</p>
          <p class="in">(8) for a political fund, return general treasury money transferred to a separate depository to the general depository of the association; and</p>
          <p>(9) make disbursements for electioneering communications.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.121.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Penalty.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) An independent expenditure political committee, independent expenditure political fund, ballot question political committee, or ballot question political fund is subject to a civil penalty of up to four times the amount of the contribution or approved expenditure if it does the following:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) makes a contribution to a candidate, local candidate, party unit, political committee, or political fund other than an independent expenditure political committee, an independent expenditure political fund, ballot question political committee, or ballot question political fund; or</p>
          <p class="in">(2) makes an approved expenditure.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) No other penalty provided in law may be imposed for conduct that is subject to a civil penalty under this section.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.10A.121.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/397/#laws.0.6.0">2010 c 397 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/138/#laws.1.23.0">2013 c 138 art 1 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/31/#laws.4.21.0">2021 c 31 art 4 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/62/#laws.5.24.0">2023 c 62 art 5 s 24</a>,25</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.10A.13">
