          <p>(c) Regardless of when made, a contribution made by a lobbyist, political committee, or political fund in order to attend an event that occurs during a regular session of the legislature and that is held by the principal campaign committee of a candidate for the legislature or constitutional office, or by a political party organization within a body of the legislature, is a violation of this section.</p>
          <p>(d) Regardless of when made, a contribution from a lobbyist, political committee, or political fund for membership or access to a facility operated during the regular session of the legislature by the principal campaign committee of a candidate for the legislature or constitutional office, or by a political party organization within a body of the legislature, is a violation of this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.273.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Party unit solicitations.</span></h2>
          <p>A political party unit must not solicit or receive at an event hosted by a candidate for the legislature or by a candidate for constitutional office a contribution from a lobbyist, political committee, political fund, or party unit during a regular session of the legislature.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.273.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Definition.</span></h2>
          <p>For purposes of this section, a "regular session" starts at 12:00 a.m., on the first day of each annual session and ends at 11:59 p.m. on the last day of each annual session. For purposes of this section, regular session does not include a special session or the interim between the two annual sessions of a biennium.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.273.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Civil penalty.</span></h2>
          <p>A candidate, political committee, party unit, political fund, an association not registered with the board, or a registered lobbyist that violates this section is subject to a civil penalty imposed by the board of up to $1,000. If the board makes a public finding that there is probable cause to believe a violation of this section has occurred, the board may bring an action, or transmit the finding to a county attorney who must bring an action, in the District Court of Ramsey County, to collect a civil penalty as imposed by the board. Penalties paid under this section must be deposited in the general fund in the state treasury.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.273.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Special election.</span></h2>
          <p>This section does not apply in a legislative special election during the period beginning when the person becomes a candidate in the special election and ending on the day of the special election.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.10A.273.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/608/#laws.3.4.0">1990 c 608 art 3 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/349/#laws.0.9.0">1991 c 349 s 9</a>,10; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/318/#laws.2.6.0">1993 c 318 art 2 s 6</a>,7; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/377/#laws.0.3.0">1994 c 377 s 3</a>,4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/220/#laws.0.8.0">1999 c 220 s 8</a>-10,50; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/363/#laws.0.31.0">2002 c 363 s 31</a>-33; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/138/#laws.3.7.0">2013 c 138 art 3 s 7</a>,8; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/73/#laws.0.16.0">2015 c 73 s 16</a>,17; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2018/0/119/#laws.0.30.0">2018 c 119 s 30</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/62/#laws.5.40.0">2023 c 62 art 5 s 40</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.10A.275">
        <h1 class="shn">10A.275 MULTICANDIDATE POLITICAL PARTY EXPENDITURES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.275.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Exceptions.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding other provisions of this chapter, the following expenditures by a party unit, or two or more party units acting together are not considered contributions to or expenditures on behalf of a candidate for the purposes of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10A.25">10A.25</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10A.27">10A.27</a> and must not be allocated to candidates under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10A.20#stat.10A.20.3">10A.20, subdivision 3</a>, paragraph (h):</p>
          <p>(1) expenditures on behalf of candidates of that party generally without referring to any of them specifically in a published, posted, or broadcast advertisement;</p>
          <p>(2) expenditures for the preparation, display, mailing, or other distribution of an official party sample ballot listing the names of three or more individuals whose names are to appear on the ballot;</p>
          <p>(3) expenditures for a telephone call, voice mail, text message, multimedia message, Internet chat message, or email when the communication includes the names of three or more individuals whose names are to appear on the ballot;</p>
          <p>(4) expenditures for a booth at a community event, county fair, or state fair that benefits three or more individuals whose names are to appear on the ballot;</p>
          <p>(5) expenditures for a political party fundraising effort on behalf of three or more candidates; or</p>
          <p>(6) expenditures for party committee staff services that benefit three or more candidates.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.275.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/220/#laws.0.51.0">1999 c 220 s 51</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.275.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/220/#laws.0.51.0">1999 c 220 s 51</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.10A.275.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/463/#laws.0.83.0">1978 c 463 s 83</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/216/#laws.1.1.0">1983 c 216 art 1 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/608/#laws.3.19.0">1990 c 608 art 3 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/220/#laws.0.35.0">1999 c 220 s 35</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/31/#laws.4.30.0">2021 c 31 art 4 s 30</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/62/#laws.5.41.0">2023 c 62 art 5 s 41</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">EXPENDITURES AND CONTRIBUTIONS; PENALTIES</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.10A.28">
        <h1 class="shn">10A.28 PENALTY FOR EXCEEDING LIMITS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.28.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Exceeding expenditure limits.</span></h2>
          <p>A candidate subject to the expenditure limits in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10A.25">10A.25</a> who permits the candidate's principal campaign committee to make expenditures or permits approved expenditures to be made on the candidate's behalf in excess of the limits imposed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10A.25">10A.25</a>, as adjusted by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10A.255">10A.255</a>, is subject to a civil penalty up to four times the amount by which the expenditures exceeded the limit.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.28.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Exceeding contribution limits.</span></h2>
          <p>The following are subject to a civil penalty of up to four times the amount by which a contribution exceeds the applicable limits:</p>
          <p>(1) a lobbyist, political committee, or political fund that makes a contribution in excess of the limits imposed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10A.27">10A.27</a>, subdivisions 1 and 8;</p>
          <p>(2) a principal campaign committee that makes a contribution in excess of the limits imposed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10A.27#stat.10A.27.2">10A.27, subdivision 2</a>;</p>
          <p>(3) a political party unit that makes a contribution in excess of the limits imposed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10A.27">10A.27</a>, subdivisions 2 and 8; or</p>
          <p>(4) a candidate who permits the candidate's principal campaign committee to accept contributions in excess of the limits imposed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10A.27">10A.27</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.28.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Conciliation agreement.</span></h2>
          <p>If the board finds that there is reason to believe that excess expenditures have been made or excess contributions accepted contrary to subdivision 1 or 2, the board must make every effort for a period of at least 14 days after its finding to correct the matter by informal methods of conference and conciliation and to enter a conciliation agreement with the person involved. A conciliation agreement under this subdivision is a matter of public record. Unless violated, a conciliation agreement is a bar to any civil proceeding under subdivision 4.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.28.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Civil action.</span></h2>
          <p>If the board is unable after a reasonable time to correct by informal methods a matter where there is reason to believe that excess expenditures have been made or excess contributions accepted contrary to subdivision 1 or 2, the board must make a public finding in the matter. The board may use section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10A.34">10A.34</a> to recover fees and penalties or to seek an injunction.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.10A.28.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/470/#laws.0.28.0">1974 c 470 s 28</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/271/#laws.0.6.0">1975 c 271 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/463/#laws.0.84.0">1978 c 463 s 84</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/608/#laws.3.20.0">1990 c 608 art 3 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/318/#laws.2.32.0">1993 c 318 art 2 s 32</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/220/#laws.0.36.0">1999 c 220 s 36</a>,50; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/363/#laws.0.34.0">2002 c 363 s 34</a>-36; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/156/#laws.6.8.0">2005 c 156 art 6 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/309/#laws.0.21.0">2014 c 309 s 21</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.10A.29">
        <h1 class="shn">10A.29 CIRCUMVENTION PROHIBITED.</h1>
        <p>An individual or association that attempts to circumvent this chapter by redirecting a contribution through, or making a contribution on behalf of, another individual or association is guilty of a gross misdemeanor and subject to a civil penalty imposed by the board of up to $3,000.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.10A.29.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/470/#laws.0.29.0">1974 c 470 s 29</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/463/#laws.0.85.0">1978 c 463 s 85</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/220/#laws.0.37.0">1999 c 220 s 37</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/363/#laws.0.37.0">2002 c 363 s 37</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">PUBLIC SUBSIDY PROGRAM</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.10A.30">
        <h1 class="shn">10A.30 STATE ELECTIONS CAMPAIGN ACCOUNT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.30.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Establishment.</span></h2>
          <p>An account is established in the special revenue fund of the state known as the "state elections campaign account."</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.30.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Separate account.</span></h2>
          <p>Within the state elections campaign account there must be maintained a separate political party account for the state committee and the candidates of each political party and a general account.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.30.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Special elections account.</span></h2>
          <p>An account is established in the special revenue fund of the state known as the "state special elections campaign account."</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.10A.30.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/470/#laws.0.30.0">1974 c 470 s 30</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/307/#laws.0.25.0">1976 c 307 s 25</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/463/#laws.0.86.0">1978 c 463 s 86</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/608/#laws.3.21.0">1990 c 608 art 3 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/349/#laws.0.18.0">1991 c 349 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/220/#laws.0.38.0">1999 c 220 s 38</a>,50; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/138/#laws.3.9.0">2013 c 138 art 3 s 9</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.10A.31">
        <h1 class="shn">10A.31 DESIGNATION OF INCOME TAX PAYMENTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.31.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Designation.</span></h2>
          <p>An individual resident of this state who files an income tax return or a renter and homeowner property tax refund return with the commissioner of revenue may designate on their original return that $5 be paid from the general fund of the state into the state elections campaign account. If spouses file a joint return, each spouse may designate that $5 be paid. No individual is allowed to designate $5 more than once in any year. The taxpayer may designate that the amount be paid into the account of a political party or into the general account.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.31.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed by amendment, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/220/#laws.0.39.0">1999 c 220 s 39</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.31.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Form.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of revenue must provide on the first page of the income tax form and the renter and homeowner property tax refund return a space for the individual to indicate a wish to pay $5 ($10 if filing a joint return) from the general fund of the state to finance election campaigns. The form must also contain language prepared by the commissioner that permits the individual to direct the state to pay the $5 (or $10 if filing a joint return) to: (1) one of the major political parties; (2) any minor political party that qualifies under subdivision 3a; or (3) all qualifying candidates as provided by subdivision 7. The renter and homeowner property tax refund return must include instructions that the individual filing the return may designate $5 on the return only if the individual has not designated $5 on the income tax return.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.31.3a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3a.<span class="headnote">Qualification of political parties.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A major political party qualifies for inclusion on the income tax form and property tax refund return as provided in subdivision 3 if it qualifies as a major political party by July 1 of the taxable year.</p>
          <p>(b) A minor political party qualifies for inclusion on the income tax form and property tax refund return as provided in subdivision 3 if it qualifies as a minor party statewide by July 1 of the taxable year.</p>
          <p>(c) The secretary of state shall notify each major and minor political party by the first Monday in January of each odd-numbered year of the conditions necessary for the party to participate in income tax form and property tax refund return programs.</p>
          <p>(d) The secretary of state shall notify each political party, the commissioner of revenue, and the Campaign Finance and Public Disclosure Board by July 1 of each year and following certification of the results of each general election of the political parties that qualify for inclusion on the income tax form and property tax refund return as provided in subdivision 3.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.31.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Appropriation.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The amounts designated by individuals for the state elections campaign account, less three percent, are appropriated from the general fund, must be transferred and credited to the appropriate account in the state elections campaign account, and are annually appropriated for distribution as set forth in subdivisions 5, 5a, 6, and 7. The remaining three percent must be kept in the general fund for administrative costs.</p>
          <p>(b) In addition to the amounts in paragraph (a), $2,432,000 for each general election is appropriated from the general fund for transfer to the general account of the state elections campaign account.</p>
          <p class="see_note">[See Note.]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.31.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Allocation.</span></h2>
          <p>(a)<b> General account.</b> In each calendar year the money in the general account must be allocated to candidates as follows:</p>
          <p>(1) 21 percent for the offices of governor and lieutenant governor together;</p>
          <p>(2) 4.2 percent for the office of attorney general;</p>
          <p>(3) 2.4 percent each for the offices of secretary of state and state auditor;</p>
          <p>(4) in each calendar year during the period in which state senators serve a four-year term, 23-1/3 percent for the office of state senator, and 46-2/3 percent for the office of state representative; and</p>
          <p>(5) in each calendar year during the period in which state senators serve a two-year term, 35 percent each for the offices of state senator and state representative.</p>
          <p>(b) <b>Party account.</b> In each calendar year the money in each party account must be allocated as follows:</p>
          <p>(1) 14 percent for the offices of governor and lieutenant governor together;</p>
          <p>(2) 2.8 percent for the office of attorney general;</p>
          <p>(3) 1.6 percent each for the offices of secretary of state and state auditor;</p>
          <p>(4) in each calendar year during the period in which state senators serve a four-year term, 23-1/3 percent for the office of state senator, and 46-2/3 percent for the office of state representative;</p>
          <p>(5) in each calendar year during the period in which state senators serve a two-year term, 35 percent each for the offices of state senator and state representative; and</p>
          <p>(6) ten percent or $50,000, whichever is less, for the state committee of a political party; one-third of any amount in excess of that allocated to the state committee of a political party under this clause must be allocated to the office of state senator and two-thirds must be allocated to the office of state representative under clause (4).</p>
          <p>Money allocated to each state committee under clause (6) must be deposited in a separate account and must be spent for only those items enumerated in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10A.275">10A.275</a>. Money allocated to a state committee under clause (6) must be paid to the committee by the board as it is received in the account on a monthly basis, with payment on the 15th day of the calendar month following the month in which the returns were processed by the Department of Revenue, provided that these distributions would be equal to 90 percent of the amount of money indicated in the Department of Revenue's weekly unedited reports of income tax returns and property tax refund returns processed in the month, as notified by the Department of Revenue to the board. The amounts paid to each state committee are subject to biennial adjustment and settlement at the time of each certification required of the commissioner of revenue under subdivisions 7 and 10. If the total amount of payments received by a state committee for the period reflected on a certification by the Department of Revenue is different from the amount that should have been received during the period according to the certification, each subsequent monthly payment must be increased or decreased to the fullest extent possible until the amount of the overpayment is recovered or the underpayment is distributed.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.31.5a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5a.<span class="headnote">Party account for legislative candidates.</span></h2>
          <p>To ensure that money will be returned to the counties from which it was collected and to ensure that the distribution of money rationally relates to the support for particular parties or for particular candidates within legislative districts, money from the party accounts for legislative candidates must be distributed as provided in this subdivision.</p>
          <p>Each candidate for the state senate and state house of representatives whose name is to appear on the ballot in the general election must receive money from the candidate's party account allocated to candidates for the state senate or state house of representatives, whichever applies, according to the following formula:</p>
          <p>For each county within the candidate's district, the candidate's share of the dollars designated by taxpayers who resided in that county and credited to the candidate's party account and allocated to that office must be:</p>
          <p>(1) the sum of the votes cast in the last general election in that part of the county in the candidate's district for all candidates of that candidate's party whose names appeared on the ballot statewide and for the state senate and state house of representatives, divided by</p>
          <p>(2) the sum of the votes cast in the entire county in the last general election for all candidates of that candidate's party whose names appeared on the ballot statewide and for the state senate and state house of representatives, multiplied by</p>
          <p>(3) the amount in the candidate's party account designated by taxpayers who resided in that county and allocated to that office.</p>
          <p>The sum of all the county shares calculated in the formula above is the candidate's share of the candidate's party account.</p>
          <p>In a year in which an election for the state senate occurs, with respect to votes for candidates for the state senate only, "last general election" means the last general election in which an election for the state senate occurred.</p>
          <p>For a party under whose name no candidate's name appeared on the ballot statewide in the last general election, amounts in the party's account must be allocated based on (i) the number of people voting in the last general election in that part of the county in the candidate's district, divided by (ii) the number of the people voting in the entire county in the last general election, multiplied by (iii) the amount in the candidate's party account designated by taxpayers who resided in that county and allocated to that office.</p>
          <p>In the first general election after the legislature is redistricted, "the candidate's district" means the newly drawn district and voting data from the last general election must be applied to the area encompassing the newly drawn district, notwithstanding that the area was in a different district in the last general election.</p>
          <p>If in a district there was no candidate of a party for the state senate or state house of representatives in the last general election, or if a candidate for the state senate or state house of representatives was unopposed, the vote for that office for that party is the average vote of all the remaining candidates of that party in each county of that district whose votes are included in the sums in clauses (1) and (2). The average vote must be added to the sums in clauses (1) and (2) before the calculation is made for all districts in the county.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.31.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Distribution of party accounts.</span></h2>
          <p>As soon as the board has obtained from the secretary of state the results of the primary election, but no later than one week after certification by the State Canvassing Board of the results of the primary, the board must distribute the available money in each party account, as certified by the commissioner of revenue one week before the state primary, to the candidates of that party who have signed a spending limit agreement under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10A.322">10A.322</a> and filed the affidavit of contributions required by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10A.323">10A.323</a>, who were opposed in either the primary election or the general election, and whose names are to appear on the ballot in the general election, according to the allocations set forth in subdivisions 5 and 5a. The public subsidy from the party account may not be paid in an amount greater than the expenditure limit of the candidate or the expenditure limit that would have applied to the candidate if the candidate had not been freed from expenditure limits under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10A.25#stat.10A.25.10">10A.25, subdivision 10</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.31.6a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6a.<span class="headnote">Party account money not distributed.</span></h2>
          <p>Money from a party account not distributed to candidates for state senator or representative in any election year must be returned to the general fund of the state, except that the subsidy from the party account an unopposed candidate would otherwise have been eligible to receive must be paid to the state committee of the candidate's political party to be deposited in a special account under subdivision 5, paragraph (b), clause (6), and used for only those items permitted under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10A.275">10A.275</a>. Money from a party account not distributed to candidates for other offices in an election year must be returned to the party account for reallocation to candidates as provided in subdivision 5, paragraph (b), in the following year.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.31.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Distribution of general account.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) As soon as the board has obtained the results of the primary election from the secretary of state, but no later than one week after certification of the primary results by the State Canvassing Board, the board must distribute the available money in the general account, as certified by the commissioner of revenue one week before the state primary and according to allocations set forth in subdivision 5, in equal amounts to all candidates of a major political party whose names are to appear on the ballot in the general election and who:</p>
          <p>(1) have signed a spending limit agreement under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10A.322">10A.322</a>;</p>
          <p>(2) have filed the affidavit of contributions required by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10A.323">10A.323</a>; and</p>
          <p>(3) were opposed in either the primary election or the general election.</p>
          <p>(b) The public subsidy under this subdivision may not be paid in an amount that would cause the sum of the public subsidy paid from the party account plus the public subsidy paid from the general account to exceed 50 percent of the expenditure limit for the candidate or 50 percent of the expenditure limit that would have applied to the candidate if the candidate had not been freed from expenditure limits under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10A.25#stat.10A.25.10">10A.25, subdivision 10</a>. Money from the general account not paid to a candidate because of the 50 percent limit must be distributed equally among all other qualifying candidates for the same office until all have reached the 50 percent limit or the balance in the general account is exhausted.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.31.7a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7a.<span class="headnote">Withholding of public subsidy.</span></h2>
          <p>If a candidate who is eligible for payment of public subsidy under this section has not filed the report of receipts and expenditures required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10A.20">10A.20</a> before a primary election, any public subsidy for which that candidate is eligible must be withheld by the board until the candidate complies with the filing requirements of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10A.20">10A.20</a> and the board has sufficient time to review or audit the report. If a candidate who is eligible for public subsidy does not file the report due before the primary election under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10A.20">10A.20</a> by the date that the report of receipts and expenditures filed before the general election is due, that candidate shall not be paid public subsidy for that election.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.31.7b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7b.<span class="headnote">Failure to repay.</span></h2>
          <p>A candidate who fails to repay money required by the agreement cannot be paid additional public subsidy funds during the current or future election cycles until the entirety of the unexpended funds and any associated collection fees are either repaid to the board or discharged by court action.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.31.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/318/#laws.2.51.0">1993 c 318 art 2 s 51</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.31.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/318/#laws.2.51.0">1993 c 318 art 2 s 51</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.31.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">December distribution.</span></h2>
          <p>In the event that on the date of either certification by the commissioner of revenue as provided in subdivision 6 or 7, less than 98 percent of the tax returns have been processed, the commissioner of revenue must certify to the board by December 1 the amount accumulated in each account since the previous certification. By December 15, the board must distribute to each candidate according to the allocations in subdivisions 5 and 5a the amounts to which the candidates are entitled.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.31.10a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10a.<span class="headnote">Form of distribution.</span></h2>
          <p>A distribution to a candidate must be in the form of a check made "payable to the campaign fund of ......(name of candidate)......."</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.31.10b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10b.<span class="headnote">Remainder.</span></h2>
          <p>Money accumulated after the final certification must be kept in the respective accounts for distribution in the next general election year.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.31.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Write-in candidate.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purposes of this section, a write-in candidate is a candidate only upon complying with sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10A.322">10A.322</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10A.323">10A.323</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.31.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed by amendment, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/220/#laws.0.39.0">1999 c 220 s 39</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.10A.31.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/470/#laws.0.31.0">1974 c 470 s 31</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/271/#laws.0.6.0">1975 c 271 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/307/#laws.0.26.0">1976 c 307 s 26</a>-33; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/463/#laws.0.87.0">1978 c 463 s 87</a>-95; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/587/#laws.3.4.0">1980 c 587 art 3 s 4</a>-6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/343/#laws.0.1.0">1981 c 343 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/523/#laws.5.1.0">1982 c 523 art 5 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/216/#laws.1.2.0">1983 c 216 art 1 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/502/#laws.2.1.0">1984 c 502 art 2 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/514/#laws.2.1.0">1984 c 514 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/248/#laws.0.3.0">1985 c 248 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/14/#laws.1.1.0">1Sp1985 c 14 art 1 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/268/#laws.1.1.0">1987 c 268 art 1 s 1</a>-3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/686/#laws.1.42.0">1988 c 686 art 1 s 42</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/1/1/#laws.10.1.0">1Sp1989 c 1 art 10 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/480/#laws.5.1.0">1990 c 480 art 5 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/199/#laws.2.1.0">1991 c 199 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/349/#laws.0.19.0">1991 c 349 s 19</a>,20; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/513/#laws.3.20.0">1992 c 513 art 3 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/13/#laws.2.1.0">1993 c 13 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/318/#laws.2.33.0">1993 c 318 art 2 s 33</a>-36; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/1/3/#laws.0.3.0">1Sp1993 c 3 s 3</a>,4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/471/#laws.1.1.0">1996 c 471 art 1 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/202/#laws.2.63.0">1997 c 202 art 2 s 63</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/220/#laws.0.39.0">1999 c 220 s 39</a>,50; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/467/#laws.0.1.0">2000 c 467 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/10/#laws.18.1.0">1Sp2001 c 10 art 18 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/363/#laws.0.38.0">2002 c 363 s 38</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/277/#laws.0.1.0">2004 c 277 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/156/#laws.6.9.0">2005 c 156 art 6 s 9</a>,10; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.18.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/184/#laws.0.1.0">2010 c 184 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/327/#laws.0.20.0">2010 c 327 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/138/#laws.3.10.0">2013 c 138 art 3 s 10</a>; art 4 s 3,4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/4/#laws.3.15.0">1Sp2017 c 4 art 3 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/62/#laws.1.50.0">2023 c 62 art 1 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/101/#laws.3.2.0">2024 c 101 art 3 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <p><b>NOTE: </b>The amendment to subdivision 4 by Laws 2023, chapter 62, article 1, section 50, is effective July 1, 2025. Laws 2023, chapter 62, article 1, section 50, the effective date.</p>
      <div class="section" id="stat.10A.315">
        <h1 class="shn">10A.315 SPECIAL ELECTION SUBSIDY.</h1>
        <p>(a) Each eligible candidate for a legislative office in a special election must be paid a public subsidy equal to the sum of:</p>
        <p>(1) the party account money at the last general election for the candidate's party for the office the candidate is seeking; and</p>
        <p>(2) the general account money paid to a candidate for the same office at the last general election.</p>
        <p>(b) A candidate who wishes to receive this public subsidy must submit a signed agreement under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10A.322">10A.322</a> to the board and must meet the contribution requirements of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10A.323">10A.323</a>. The special election subsidy must be distributed in the same manner as money in the party and general accounts is distributed to legislative candidates in a general election.</p>
        <p>(c) The amount necessary to make the payments required by this section is appropriated from the general fund for transfer to the state special elections campaign account for distribution by the board as set forth in this section.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.10A.315.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/608/#laws.3.22.0">1990 c 608 art 3 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/318/#laws.2.38.0">1993 c 318 art 2 s 38</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/220/#laws.0.40.0">1999 c 220 s 40</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/138/#laws.3.11.0">2013 c 138 art 3 s 11</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.10A.316"><b>10A.316</b> [Never effective]</div>
      <div class="sr_by_subd" id="stat.10A.32">
        <h1>10A.32 </h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.32.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/608/#laws.3.32.0">1990 c 608 art 3 s 32</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.32.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/608/#laws.3.32.0">1990 c 608 art 3 s 32</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.32.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/608/#laws.3.32.0">1990 c 608 art 3 s 32</a>]</p>

          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3a.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/608/#laws.3.32.0">1990 c 608 art 3 s 32</a>]</p>

      <div class="section" id="stat.10A.321">
        <h1 class="shn">10A.321 ESTIMATES OF MINIMUM AMOUNTS TO BE RECEIVED.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.321.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Calculation and certification of estimates.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of revenue must calculate and certify to the board one week before the first day for filing for office in each election year an estimate of the total amount in the state general account of the state elections campaign account and the amount of money each candidate who qualifies, as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10A.31">10A.31</a>, subdivisions 6 and 7, may receive from the candidate's party account in the state elections campaign account. This estimate must be based upon the allocations and formulas in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10A.31">10A.31</a>, subdivisions 5 and 5a, any necessary vote totals provided by the secretary of state to apply the formulas in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10A.31">10A.31</a>, subdivisions 5 and 5a, and the amount of money expected to be available after 100 percent of the tax returns have been processed.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.321.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Publication, certification, and notification procedures.</span></h2>
          <p>Before the first day of filing for office, the board must publish and forward to all filing officers the estimates calculated and certified under subdivision 1 along with a copy of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10A.25#stat.10A.25.10">10A.25, subdivision 10</a>. Within one week after the last day for filing for office, the secretary of state must certify to the board the name, address, office sought, and party affiliation of each candidate who has filed with that office an affidavit of candidacy or petition to appear on the ballot. The auditor of each county must certify to the board the same information for each candidate who has filed with that county an affidavit of candidacy or petition to appear on the ballot. Within two weeks after the last day for filing for office, the board must notify all candidates of their estimated minimum amount. The board must include with the notice a form for the agreement provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10A.322">10A.322</a> along with a copy of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10A.25#stat.10A.25.10">10A.25, subdivision 10</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.10A.321.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/608/#laws.3.23.0">1990 c 608 art 3 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/220/#laws.0.41.0">1999 c 220 s 41</a>,50; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/184/#laws.0.2.0">2010 c 184 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/138/#laws.4.5.0">2013 c 138 art 4 s 5</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.10A.322">
        <h1 class="shn">10A.322 SPENDING LIMIT AGREEMENTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.322.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Agreement by candidate.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) As a condition of receiving a public subsidy, a candidate must sign and file with the board a written agreement in which the candidate agrees that the candidate will comply with sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10A.25">10A.25</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10A.27#stat.10A.27.10">10A.27, subdivision 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10A.324">10A.324</a>; and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10A.38">10A.38</a>.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) Before the first day of filing for office, the board must forward agreement forms to all filing officers. The board must also provide agreement forms to candidates on request at any time. The candidate must file the agreement with the board at least three weeks before the candidate's state primary. An agreement may not be filed after that date. An agreement once filed may not be rescinded.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) The board must notify the commissioner of revenue of any agreement signed under this subdivision.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) Notwithstanding paragraph (b), if a vacancy occurs that will be filled by means of a special election and the filing period does not coincide with the filing period for the general election, a candidate may sign and submit a spending limit agreement not later than the day after the close of the filing period for the special election for which the candidate filed.</p>
          <p>(e) Notwithstanding paragraphs (b) and (d), if a vacancy occurs that will be filled by means of a special election called under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/204B.13#stat.204B.13.2">204B.13, subdivision 2</a>, paragraph (c), a candidate may sign and submit a spending limit agreement not later than eight calendar days after the general election.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.322.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">How long agreement is effective.</span></h2>
          <p>The agreement, insofar as it relates to the expenditure limits in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10A.25">10A.25</a>, as adjusted by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10A.255">10A.255</a>, and the contribution limit in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10A.27#stat.10A.27.10">10A.27, subdivision 10</a>, remains effective for candidates until the dissolution of the principal campaign committee of the candidate or the end of the first election cycle completed after the agreement was filed, whichever occurs first.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.322.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed by amendment, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/220/#laws.0.42.0">1999 c 220 s 42</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.322.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Refund receipt forms; penalty.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The board must make available to a political party on request and to any candidate for whom an agreement under this section is effective, a supply of official refund receipt forms that state in boldface type that:</p>
          <p>(1) a contributor who is given a receipt form is eligible to claim a refund as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.06#stat.290.06.23">290.06, subdivision 23</a>; and</p>
          <p>(2) if the contribution is to a candidate, that the candidate has signed an agreement to limit campaign expenditures as provided in this section.</p>
          <p class="lj">The forms must provide duplicate copies of the receipt to be attached to the contributor's claim.</p>
          <p>(b) The willful issuance of an official refund receipt form or a facsimile of one to any of the candidate's contributors by a candidate or treasurer of a candidate who did not sign an agreement under this section is subject to a civil penalty of up to $3,000 imposed by the board.</p>
          <p>(c) The willful issuance of an official refund receipt form or a facsimile to an individual not eligible to claim a refund under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.06#stat.290.06.23">290.06, subdivision 23</a>, is subject to a civil penalty of up to $3,000 imposed by the board.</p>
          <p>(d) A violation of paragraph (b) or (c) is a misdemeanor.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.10A.322.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/608/#laws.3.24.0">1990 c 608 art 3 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/291/#laws.6.1.0">1991 c 291 art 6 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/318/#laws.2.39.0">1993 c 318 art 2 s 39</a>,40; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/220/#laws.0.42.0">1999 c 220 s 42</a>,50; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/363/#laws.0.39.0">2002 c 363 s 39</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/295/#laws.0.6.0">2008 c 295 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/184/#laws.0.3.0">2010 c 184 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/327/#laws.0.21.0">2010 c 327 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/138/#laws.3.12.0">2013 c 138 art 3 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/275/#laws.1.2.0">2014 c 275 art 1 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/73/#laws.0.18.0">2015 c 73 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2018/0/119/#laws.0.31.0">2018 c 119 s 31</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.10A.323">
        <h1 class="shn">10A.323 AFFIDAVIT OF CONTRIBUTIONS.</h1>
        <p class="in">(a) In addition to the requirements of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10A.322">10A.322</a>, to be eligible to receive a public subsidy under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10A.31">10A.31</a> a candidate or the candidate's treasurer must:</p>
        <p class="in">(1) between January 1 of the previous year and the cutoff date for transactions included in the report of receipts and expenditures due before the primary election, accumulate contributions from individuals eligible to vote in this state in at least the amount indicated for the office sought, counting only the first $50 received from each contributor, excluding in-kind contributions:</p>
        <p class="in">(i) candidates for governor and lieutenant governor running together, $35,000;</p>
        <p class="in">(ii) candidates for attorney general, $15,000;</p>
        <p class="in">(iii) candidates for secretary of state and state auditor, separately, $6,000;</p>
        <p class="in">(iv) candidates for the senate, $3,000; and</p>
        <p class="in">(v) candidates for the house of representatives, $1,500;</p>
        <p class="in">(2) file an affidavit with the board stating that the principal campaign committee has complied with this paragraph. The affidavit must state the total amount of contributions that have been received from individuals eligible to vote in this state, excluding:</p>
        <p class="in">(i) the portion of any contribution in excess of $50;</p>
        <p>(ii) any in-kind contribution; and</p>
        <p>(iii) any contribution for which the name and address of the contributor is not known and recorded; and</p>
        <p class="in">(3) submit the affidavit required by this section to the board in writing by the deadline for reporting of receipts and expenditures before a primary under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10A.20">10A.20</a>, subdivision 2.</p>
        <p class="in">(b) A candidate for a vacancy to be filled at a special election for which the filing period does not coincide with the filing period for the general election must accumulate the contributions specified in paragraph (a) and must submit the affidavit required by this section to the board within five days after the close of the filing period for the special election for which the candidate filed.</p>
        <p>(c) Notwithstanding paragraphs (a) and (b), a candidate for a vacancy to be filled at a special election called under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/204B.13#stat.204B.13.2">204B.13, subdivision 2</a>, paragraph (c), must accumulate the contributions specified in paragraph (a) and must submit the affidavit required by this section to the board within 12 calendar days after the general election.</p>
        <p>(d) A candidate or the candidate's treasurer must be able to electronically file the affidavit required under this section in the same manner as other reports required by this chapter. The board must not require the candidate or candidate's treasurer to notarize the affidavit of contribution.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.10A.323.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/608/#laws.3.25.0">1990 c 608 art 3 s 25</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/318/#laws.2.41.0">1993 c 318 art 2 s 41</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/254/#laws.1.1.0">1998 c 254 art 1 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/220/#laws.0.43.0">1999 c 220 s 43</a>,50; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/363/#laws.0.40.0">2002 c 363 s 40</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/295/#laws.0.7.0">2008 c 295 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/184/#laws.0.4.0">2010 c 184 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/327/#laws.0.22.0">2010 c 327 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/397/#laws.0.14.0">2010 c 397 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/138/#laws.1.50.0">2013 c 138 art 1 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/4/#laws.3.16.0">1Sp2017 c 4 art 3 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2018/0/119/#laws.0.32.0">2018 c 119 s 32</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/31/#laws.4.31.0">2021 c 31 art 4 s 31</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.10A.324">
        <h1 class="shn">10A.324 RETURN OF PUBLIC SUBSIDY.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.324.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">When return required.</span></h2>
          <p>A candidate must return all or a portion of the public subsidy received from the state elections campaign account or the public subsidy received under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10A.315">10A.315</a>, under the circumstances in this section or section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10A.257#stat.10A.257.1">10A.257, subdivision 1</a>.</p>
          <p>To the extent that the amount of public subsidy received exceeds the aggregate of: (1) actual expenditures made by the principal campaign committee of the candidate; and (2) approved expenditures made on behalf of the candidate, the treasurer of the candidate's principal campaign committee must return an amount equal to the difference to the board. The cost of postage that was not used during an election cycle and payments that created credit balances at vendors at the close of an election cycle are not considered expenditures for purposes of determining the amount to be returned. Expenditures in excess of the candidate's spending limit do not count in determining aggregate expenditures under this paragraph.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.324.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/220/#laws.0.51.0">1999 c 220 s 51</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.324.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">How return determined.</span></h2>
          <p>Whether or not a candidate is required under subdivision 1 to return all or a portion of the public subsidy must be determined from the report required to be filed with the board by that candidate by January 31 of the year following an election. An amount required to be returned must be submitted in the form of a check or money order and must accompany the report filed with the board. The board must deposit the check or money order in the state treasury for credit to the general fund. The amount returned must not exceed the amount of public subsidy received by the candidate.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.324.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/220/#laws.0.51.0">1999 c 220 s 51</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.324.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/459/#laws.0.5.0">1996 c 459 s 5</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.10A.324.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/608/#laws.3.26.0">1990 c 608 art 3 s 26</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/349/#laws.0.21.0">1991 c 349 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/318/#laws.2.42.0">1993 c 318 art 2 s 42</a>-44; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/220/#laws.0.44.0">1999 c 220 s 44</a>,45,50; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/138/#laws.4.6.0">2013 c 138 art 4 s 6</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.10A.325"><b>10A.325</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/220/#laws.0.51.0">1999 c 220 s 51</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.10A.33"><b>10A.33</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/608/#laws.3.32.0">1990 c 608 art 3 s 32</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.10A.335"><b>10A.335</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/220/#laws.0.51.0">1999 c 220 s 51</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">MISCELLANEOUS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.10A.34">
        <h1 class="shn">10A.34 REMEDIES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.34.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Personal liability.</span></h2>
          <p>A person charged with a duty under this chapter is personally liable for the penalty for failing to discharge it.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.34.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.<span class="headnote">Recovering fees and penalties.</span></h2>
          <p>The board may bring an action in the district court in Ramsey County to recover a fee, late filing fee, or penalty imposed under this chapter. Money recovered must be deposited in the general fund of the state.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.34.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Injunction.</span></h2>
          <p>The board or a county attorney may seek an injunction in the district court to enforce this chapter.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.34.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Not a crime.</span></h2>
          <p>Unless otherwise provided, a violation of this chapter is not a crime.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.10A.34.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Penalty for violations of chapter 211B under board's jurisdiction.</span></h2>
          <p>If a civil penalty is not specified in a section of chapter 211B brought under the board's jurisdiction by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10A.022#stat.10A.022.3">10A.022, subdivision 3</a>, the board may impose a civil penalty of up to $3,000.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.10A.34.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/470/#laws.0.34.0">1974 c 470 s 34</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/271/#laws.0.6.0">1975 c 271 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/463/#laws.0.104.0">1978 c 463 s 104</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/220/#laws.0.46.0">1999 c 220 s 46</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/1/#laws.2.28.0">1Sp2003 c 1 art 2 s 28</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/73/#laws.0.19.0">2015 c 73 s 19</a>,26</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.10A.35">
        <h1 class="shn">10A.35 COMMERCIAL USE OF INFORMATION PROHIBITED.</h1>
        <p>Information copied from reports and statements filed with the board, other than reports and statements filed by lobbyists and lobbyist principals, may not be sold or used by an individual or association for a commercial purpose. Purposes related to elections, political activities, or law enforcement are not commercial purposes. An individual or association who violates this section is subject to a civil penalty of up to $1,000. An individual who knowingly violates this section is guilty of a misdemeanor.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.10A.35.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/220/#laws.0.47.0">1999 c 220 s 47</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/327/#laws.0.23.0">2010 c 327 s 23</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.10A.36">
        <h1 class="shn">10A.36 REPRISALS PROHIBITED; PENALTY.</h1>
        <p>An individual or association must not engage in economic reprisals or threaten loss of employment or physical coercion against an individual or association because of that individual's or association's political contributions or political activity. This subdivision does not apply to compensation for employment or loss of employment if the political affiliation or viewpoint of the employee is a bona fide occupational qualification of the employment. An individual or association that violates this section is guilty of a gross misdemeanor.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.10A.36.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/470/#laws.0.20.0">1974 c 470 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/271/#laws.0.6.0">1975 c 271 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/307/#laws.0.14.0">1976 c 307 s 14</a>-18; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/346/#laws.0.1.0">1977 c 346 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/463/#laws.0.51.0">1978 c 463 s 51</a>-59; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/793/#laws.0.37.0">1978 c 793 s 37</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1979/0/59/#laws.0.5.0">1979 c 59 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/587/#laws.2.9.0">1980 c 587 art 2 s 9</a>,10; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/607/#laws.17.10.0">1980 c 607 art 17 s 10</a>,11; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/40/#laws.0.1.0">1985 c 40 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/214/#laws.0.3.0">1987 c 214 s 3</a>,4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/608/#laws.3.5.0">1990 c 608 art 3 s 5</a>-7; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/349/#laws.0.11.0">1991 c 349 s 11</a>,12; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/318/#laws.2.15.0">1993 c 318 art 2 s 15</a>-18; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/459/#laws.0.1.0">1996 c 459 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/202/#laws.2.7.0">1997 c 202 art 2 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/220/#laws.0.23.0">1999 c 220 s 23</a>,50</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.10A.37">
        <h1 class="shn">10A.37 FREEDOM TO ASSOCIATE AND COMMUNICATE.</h1>
        <p>Nothing in this chapter may be construed to abridge the right of an association to communicate with its members.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.10A.37.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/463/#laws.0.75.0">1978 c 463 s 75</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/220/#laws.0.33.0">1999 c 220 s 33</a>,50</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.10A.38">
        <h1 class="shn">10A.38 CAPTIONING OF CAMPAIGN ADVERTISEMENTS.</h1>
        <p class="in">(a) This section applies to a campaign advertisement by a candidate who is governed by an agreement under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10A.322">10A.322</a>.</p>
        <p class="in">(b) "Campaign advertisement" means a professionally produced visual or audio recording of two minutes or less produced by the candidate for the purpose of influencing the nomination or election of a candidate.</p>
        <p class="in">(c) A campaign advertisement that is disseminated as an advertisement by broadcast or cable television must include closed captioning for deaf and hard-of-hearing viewers, unless the candidate has filed with the board before the advertisement is disseminated a statement setting forth the reasons for not doing so. A campaign advertisement that is disseminated as an advertisement to the public on the candidate's website must include closed captioning for deaf and hard-of-hearing viewers, unless the candidate has posted on the website a transcript of the spoken content of the advertisement or the candidate has filed with the board before the advertisement is disseminated a statement setting forth the reasons for not doing so. A campaign advertisement must not be disseminated as an advertisement by radio unless the candidate has posted on the candidate's website a transcript of the spoken content of the advertisement or the candidate has filed with the board before the advertisement is disseminated a statement setting forth the reasons for not doing so.</p>
        <p>(d) A candidate who fails to comply with the requirements of paragraph (c) is subject to a civil penalty imposed by the board of up to $1,000.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.10A.38.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/295/#laws.0.8.0">2008 c 295 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/62/#laws.5.42.0">2023 c 62 art 5 s 42</a></p>
      </div>
